# RELEASE_LOG

Per-release append log. State transitions: `DEPLOYED` (Stage 12 Phase B5) → `VERIFIED` (Stage 13 chore PR).

Spec: [`stage-12-execute.md § Phase B5`](../references/pipeline/stage-12-execute.md) (DEPLOYED row + visible-H4 Deployment Log); [`stage-13-close.md § Phase B`](../references/pipeline/stage-13-close.md) (DEPLOYED → VERIFIED transition).

**Date anchor — merge event.** The `Date` column dates the **merge of the release PR to `main`** (written at Stage 12), not the Stage-13 close-out. It is written once and never rewritten by the close. `RELEASE_INDEX.md` carries the **same** anchor and is sourced from this column; `RELEASE_DIGEST.md` and `CHANGELOG.md` carry the **close-out** anchor and may therefore differ by a day without contradicting this row. Taxonomy and format rules: [`date-variable-convention.md § Emission-Time Anchors`](../../core/standards/date-variable-convention.md). Rows predating that declaration are grandfathered — anchors are declared forward, never backfilled.

#### Deployment Log v4.47
**Files deployed:** An install-side copy **was owed and was made** — the opposite of the preceding release, and measured rather than inherited. Denominator: **68** files changed by the release merge (65 `M` / 3 `A`), measured at the merge commit against its first parent `1c857727` rather than carried over from the plan. Bucket classification: **1** touches a `skills/**` surface, **2** touch `packages/**`, **0** touch `core/rules/`, **65** other; the buckets sum to the denominator (1 + 2 + 0 + 65 = 68). The counts are measured rather than vacuous: the classifier was run against a sensitivity arm (synthetic `core/skills/**`, `operations/skills/**`, `packages/**` and `core/rules/**` paths each classify to their own bucket) and a specificity arm (a corpus-standard path and an ADR path both classify as `other`). The single `skills/**` entry is `core/skills/adr-helper/references/scaffolding-procedure.md` — a `references/` file, **not** a `SKILL.md` — and the **2** `packages/**` entries are `adr-helper.skill` with its `.sha256` content-manifest sidecar, rebuilt inside the release PR itself. Because the Delivery Strategy declares **"Git merge + S-2 skill copy + manifest execution"**, Phase B4 is **owed, not N/A**, and drift was confirmed before acting: both install roots carried `d1a99b77` against the repo's `ad8f61e8`. The copy was performed scoped to the affected skill (`deploy.sh --deploy adr-helper --release adr-corpus-status-integrity`) rather than as a roster-wide redeploy, and **verified by hash rather than by exit code** — both roots read `ad8f61e8` afterward, matching the repo, so the known silent-no-op-on-stale-baseline failure mode is excluded by measurement. Package integrity was checked with the governed rebuild-stable content-manifest algorithm (per-member `sha256  arcname`, `LC_ALL=C`-sorted, then hashed), not a raw archive hash: **4 of 4** packages match their sidecars, `adr-helper` among them, against three untouched controls. A first pass mis-read all four as MISMATCH by hashing archive bytes — a question the sidecar deliberately does not answer, since zip embeds mtimes and member order — and the error is recorded rather than quietly corrected.
**Mechanism:** git merge-commit (PR #6626 to main at `e62522ac1b1a340a5a3bef7c59e5864d62f76e13`), **versioned**, git-native. Merged with `gh pr merge --merge` — **no `--admin`**, no signing bypass, no force-push. The declared `merge` strategy was confirmed enabled on the repo (`{merge: true, squash: true, rebase: true}`) before use. **This release re-claimed after a tooling defect, and the first attempt is recorded rather than elided.** The initial Stage-12 claim pushed a malformed-but-signed tag `v.01` and exited 0: `compute_next_free()` read `anchor()` inside a command substitution and discarded its return code, so a transient failed read yielded an empty string, `version_parse` yielded nothing, and arithmetic on an empty operand evaluated to 1. The defect was fixed before re-claiming — an rc-check plus a canonicality assertion, with a `U-16(e)` arm proven to discriminate by reverting the production fix and observing the arm fail while reproducing the exact observed `v.01` value — and shipped as its own PR with 48/48 checks green. Version `v4.47` was then claimed atomically at the merge SHA by `claim-version.sh --sha e62522ac --bump minor --stamp-slug adr-corpus-status-integrity`, which won the compare-and-swap, pushed the SSH-signed annotated tag `v4.47` at that commit, and stamped the plan to `release/releases/plans/v4/v4.47_RELEASE_PLAN.md` with every `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}` token resolved (0 remain; the Version cell reads `v4.47`). The `--stamp-slug` flag was **mandatory, not optional**, here: six slug-named plans share the `plans/` depth-1 home, so auto-derivation hits an ambiguity the tool refuses rather than guesses, and omitting the flag skips the stamping pass silently on an exit-0 path. The malformed `v.01` tag **remains in the tag namespace**: the active `protect-version-tags` ruleset (`refs/tags/v*`, `deletion` rule) refuses its removal, and that control was left intact rather than relaxed or bypassed. It is inert — grammar-rejected by `version_canonical`, no published Release, no ledger row — and cannot poison anchor computation, which now additionally HALTs on a non-canonical anchor instead of deriving from one.

The unmerged sibling PR #6357 claiming ADR-163/164/165 was correctly treated as advisory and left untouched — per ADR-115 an unmerged ADR claim does not bind, and merging first settles the contention in this release's favour.
**Not-produced:** github-release — the release claims no version and no tag, and a GitHub Release cannot be published without one; publishing would have minted precisely the tag the declared version-less identity withholds. Phase B3 confirmed **0** tags at the merge SHA and **0** tags matching the release slug, measured against a live control of **372** remote tag refs, so the probe was sound rather than trivially empty. The framework guard that a merged release carrying no `DEPLOYED` row and no version tag blocks before close-out applies here in its **RELEASE_LOG half only**: this row discharges that half, and the tag half is inapplicable by design. An absent tag on this release is the correct terminal state, not a missing artifact, and this marker is evidence rather than an exemption. Re-verified at Stage 13 close: still **0** tags at the merge SHA. **Not-produced:** changelog-entry — `CHANGELOG.md` carries `## [vX.Y]` keys and this release claims no version key, so there is no key to write. This is the projector's own declared behaviour, not an omission: `generate_release_index.py --emit changelog` **refuses** a version-less release with *"is version-less and has no `## [vX.Y]` key to write … refusing to invent"*, and `assert_derived_surfaces` carries a matching legitimate N/A branch. **The corpus precedent is mixed, and is stated accurately rather than conveniently:** of the **14** version-less LOG rows, **8 carry** a `## [<slug>]` CHANGELOG section and **6 do not** — but the split is temporal, not arbitrary. Every version-less release from 2026-06-04 to 2026-06-24 carries one (8 of 9); every version-less release from **2026-07-22 onward carries none (5 of 5 consecutive)**, this one included. The earlier entries predate the projector's refusal; the current codified behaviour is the refusal, and this close follows it. A control confirms the probe detects presence rather than reading blind: **181 of 187** versioned rows do carry a `## [vX.Y]` section. **Two governed mechanisms disagree here and the disagreement is recorded, not resolved by fiat:** `deploy.sh --check-close-completeness` **demands** a `## [<slug>]` section for a version-less row and files this release as a finding, while the projector **refuses to emit one**. #### Deployment Log v4.35
**Velocity:** planned 20 pts / delivered 20 pts (1.00); files-changed 17; allocation 0/12/8 pts (feature/debt/protocol-slack); class novel; mechanism: compute-release-velocity.sh

**Mechanism:** merge-commit, reached in two steps and recorded as such. PR #5765 was first merged with `--squash` at `57f75e3abada132070edb19a820aed1aa13c854a`. That was a **deviation from the documented default**: `pipeline/stage-12-execute.md` A.4.1 states that when a release plan declares no merge strategy — and this plan's Delivery Strategy declares none — **default `gh pr merge --merge` semantics apply per existing convention**. The squash structurally voided the Per-Issue Rollback this release's own Rollback Strategy declares (*"each card's commit is revertible in isolation, in reverse dependency order"*), because ten commits collapsed into one.

**Repair (PR #5813, operator-authorised):** the squash was reverted — verified to restore the tree byte-identically to the pre-merge state `7982e279` — and the release branch at `9dcb960f` was re-merged with `--no-ff`. Net content change is **zero**: `git diff 57f75e3a f2619f5e` is empty, so the repair altered ancestry, not files. `git log --pretty=%P` on `f2619f5e` returns **two** parents, so the `git revert -m 1` first-parent rollback form the Rollback Strategy relies on applies and is intact. All five commits are verified ancestors of `main`: `c7de12fa` (#5668) · `2f315575` (#3123) · `2ed89e97` (#5741) · `2b4f9062` (operator-side Tier-0 rows) · `eeb85bf0` (package rebuild).

**Why revert-and-re-merge rather than a force-push:** two sibling milestones were in flight and may already have fetched the squash SHA; rewriting published history on a public repository to improve the shape of the log was not worth that risk. The visible revert commit is the honest record.

**Merge conduct:** plain `gh pr merge --merge`; `--admin` was neither used nor needed, consistent with the measured branch-protection posture. Final CI on the release head `9dcb960f` was **47/47 pass, 0 non-pass**; the repair PR was **40/40 pass**.

**Corpus write: NOT EXECUTED — structurally gated, by operator decision.** Two of the three cards mutate the git-ignored Layer-2 corpus, and that write is deliberately deferred. A bare `--stamp` on `main` returns `GATE REFUSED`, naming the Stage-12 window and the `--i-am-at-stage-12` token. Six keys across the Person and Portfolio tiers have no value source anywhere on the branch and carry explicit `UNSOURCED — operator input` markers under an S5 present-or-halt rule, and two target filenames remain an operator decision because `project-entity-model.md` §7 defers physicalization and ADR-138 declined to give the Portfolio record a declared producer. Inventing either would breach No-invention. **#3123's card value is the corpus write; it ships its tooling without its outcome, and that is stated here rather than closed quietly.**

**Result:** SUCCESS — release PR #5765 merged to main; the `v4.35` annotated tag was claimed at `ec62696b` via `claim-version.sh`, and the claim-time stamp fired — resolving `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}` and relocating the plan to its ADR-092 nested home. **Two Stage-12 steps were initially skipped, and both were caught by controls rather than by review.** (1) The merge used `--squash` against the documented `--merge` default, voiding the Per-Issue Rollback this release declares; repaired additively by PR #5813 — net content change zero, two parents restored, so `git revert -m 1` applies. (2) The ADR-092 atomic claim was not performed, surfaced by the close-out smoke as `PLAN-MISSING-FOR-LEDGER-ROW`; `claim-version.sh` then HALTed pre-CAS because the Stage-4 plan described the claim in prose but carried no `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}` token to resolve — a Stage-4 authoring gap the tool refused to guess past. **The Layer-2 corpus write did not execute and remains structurally gated**, tracked at #5815.
**Outcome:** SUCCESS

The intersection between that 1-file set and this branch's **20** changed paths computed as **empty**, against a sensitivity arm that injected a known-PR file and correctly returned **1** and a second arm intersecting the main-side set with a superset containing it that correctly returned **1** — so the empty result discriminates rather than failing open. `git merge-tree --write-tree` then returned exit **0** with clean tree `8c0e7a27`, against a **synthetic two-branch conflicting control that correctly returned exit 1**, so the zero is a real clean-merge signal rather than an unreachable code path. **Zero force pushes, zero rebases, zero history rewrites.** CI was read as parsed rows rather than exit status and re-polled at the merge instant: **69 check-runs, 69 SUCCESS, 0 non-passing, 0 not-COMPLETED, 0 SKIPPED**, against a control filter (the same predicate minus the SUCCESS allowance) that correctly flagged all **69**. That rollup of **69** resolves to **49 distinct check names**, with **20** names appearing exactly twice under two trigger events — which reconciles the Stage-9 recorded "49 checks" with the raw run count, so the delta is a counting-basis difference and not an expanded, new, or degraded population. Signed-annotated tag `v4.34` bound at the Stage-12 **atomic ref-CAS** via `claim-version.sh --sha 11d244bbc498 --bump minor --stamp-slug closeout-reports-what-shipped --stamp-branch chore/v4.34-stage-12-release-log` — **first-attempt CAS win, zero collisions, zero signing bypasses**; bump-class `minor` off anchor `v4.33`. **Next-free was re-derived at the claim rather than carried, and that was load-bearing here rather than ceremonial** because a sibling release entered the pipeline during the gate and would compute the same next-free: the pre-merge `--dry-run` computed `v4.34`, an independent remote probe found **0** `refs/tags/v4.34` against a `v4.33` control correctly returning **2**, and the CAS then claimed `v4.34`. No re-derivation was required, so **no `RELEASE_REVERSIONS` ledger entry is owed**. **`--stamp-slug` was passed explicitly**, derived from the merged PR's `headRefName` (`release/closeout-reports-what-shipped`, stem-stripped) rather than typed as a literal. A depth-1 sweep of `release/releases/plans/` plus `_unversioned/` across **43** candidate plans found **exactly one** carrying a live `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}` token, so the tool's own derivation would have resolved the same slug unaided — **the anticipated two-in-flight ambiguity did not materialise**, because the concurrent sibling's plan carries no such token. The stamp was verified by observation of the pushed remote rather than inferred from exit 0 — the precise failure mode a prior release shipped, in which the claim returns 0, the tag binds, and the rename silently does not happen: `release/releases/plans/v4/v4.34_RELEASE_PLAN.md` exists and is git-tracked, the rename is recorded `R099` from the flat slug-named path, the Header `**Version**` cell resolves to `v4.34`, and the stamp landed as commit `dbc4ff98` on `origin/chore/v4.34-stage-12-release-log` read back from `git ls-remote`. `git cat-file -t v4.34` returns `tag` (a real tag object, not a lightweight ref) with a **Good ED25519 signature**, and `refs/tags/v4.34^{}` read from `git ls-remote` dereferences to `11d244bbc498e0006bb68dbd6368c220c8c2d068` — tag↔SHA identity **MATCH**, read from the remote rather than the local copy, with the tagger line carrying the GitHub no-reply address. **Phase A.5.7 did not fire and the omission is the correct signal, not a skip:** the branch adds **0** `ADR-NNN-*.md` files, against a control that correctly finds **136** ADR paths in the tree. **Phase B0 found 0 dependent PRs** based on the release head branch, so `--delete-branch` carried no auto-close hazard and the release branch was deleted at merge. **Phase A.7** read `isDraft == false`. **Six** control firings occurred during the stage, enumerated rather than estimated, and each was resolved by **rewording rather than bypass**. **Five** were the worktree-isolation guard refusing a compound command — the initial scratch-directory setup (assignment + `mktemp` + a redirect resolving outside the worktree), a heredoc-based path-intersection probe, a two-statement ADR probe, the claim invocation's `$(…)` slug substitution, and a backgrounded `until`-loop check poll — each re-expressed as plain sequential commands with identical effect, with the slug still derived from the merged PR's `headRefName` rather than typed blind. The **sixth** was `block-destructive.sh` **`BLOCK-DESTRUCTIVE-022`** refusing `bash <script>` for a check-polling helper under the Red-Team-C1 script-laundering mitigation, resolved by polling inline with plain `gh pr checks` calls — **the allowlist was not edited and no bypass was set**. The count is stated as of this commit and is enumerated rather than summarised because a prior release (v4.32) shipped an inaccurate Stage-12 control-firing count and required a dedicated follow-up PR to correct it; enumerating the firings inline is what makes the number checkable rather than asserted. **`CLAUDE_HOOK_BYPASS` was never set**, no `--no-verify` was used, and nothing was pushed to `main` outside the PR merge.

**Result:** SUCCESS — release PR #5759 merged to main via a true two-parent merge commit; `v4.34` signed-annotated tag pushed at the merge SHA and verified from the origin ref; the claim-time stamp fired and was verified against the pushed remote. **The release's load-bearing integration invariant held across the merge:** `_insert_field_after_in_block` in `release/tools/automated-closeout.sh` reads **49 lines, md5 `7d0fe7cef0a38b983f2785ea6726ebc9`** on post-merge `main` — byte-identical to the release base — against a one-byte-mutation control that produced a different digest and an adjacent-window control that also differed, so the match discriminates rather than being a trivially-true comparison. The function moved from line 1886 to 2024 without changing a byte, which is exactly what CIAC-1 asserts. **Outstanding at Stage 12 close, recorded rather than silently carried:** (i) **the claim-time stamp resolved a token inside PROSE ABOUT the token, not only the live placeholder** — the § Deviation Log **DV-18** row now reads "the plan binds its version as `v4.34`" and "refuses a `v4.34` token on a `version:` line" where it had described the `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}` mechanism, so this plan's own claim of "exactly **one** such token — the Header `**Version**` cell" was false and the stamp changed **2** lines. `claim-version.sh` documents this prose-vs-placeholder blindness for its *candidate-selection* oracle (the depth-1 restriction exists to contain it); this is the same blindness one seam over, in the *resolution* pass, where no depth restriction can help. It is a documentary defect in an archived plan rather than an identity defect — the Header cell, the rename, and the tag are all correct — and it is recorded here rather than repaired in-stage because the plan is a frozen record and the repair is a Stage-13 or follow-up call. (ii) Surface 1 (the published GitHub Release) is **not yet emitted** — `release/releases/notes/v4.34_RELEASE_NOTES.md` is authored at Stage 13. (iii) the milestone and its sub-tasks remain open by design — Stage 12 closes neither. (iv) #5188 (`DROP-EMITORDER`) was removed from this release at Collective Review and closed not-a-defect, and #5244 was closed by an earlier release on 2026-08-16, before this plan existed; neither is a delivered member of this bundle, so the row lists the **5** delivered cards rather than the 7 issues the milestone carries.

#### Deployment Log v4.33
**Files deployed:** `operator-runtime-state-and-workspace-anchoring` shipped as one release PR #5632 (`release/operator-runtime-state-and-workspace-anchoring → main`, merge commit `63dcbe8e`), **56 files (+4596/−272)** against the pre-merge mainline `d9bf8517`. Full bucket census of the landed set, enumerated rather than sampled: `core/` × **34**, `release/` × **12**, `docs/` × **5**, `update.sh` × **1**, `operations/` × **1**, `CHANGELOG.md` × **1**, `.version` × **1**, `.github/` × **1** — the eight counts sum to **56**, matching the denominator exactly, so the census is complete rather than a sample that happened to find nothing. Of that set, **`core/hooks/` × 11 and `core/rules/` × 4** are the operator-owed surfaces, the same shape v4.31 recorded and the inverse of v4.32's zero.
**Mechanism:** squash merge (PR #5632 → main at `63dcbe8e3fd9c3985d7a603f0d700e2f155a66a5`; `git rev-list --parents -n 1` returns **one** parent, i.e. a single-parent squash rather than the two-parent merge commit v4.32 recorded). This is a deliberate departure from the preceding precedent and it does **not** void the plan's rollback limb, which was checked rather than assumed: the plan's Rollback Strategy names a plain `git revert` of the merge commit, not the `-m 1` first-parent form, so it applies unchanged to a single-parent commit. The plan also already distinguishes the two limbs — "`git revert` restores Layer 1. It does not restore `<workspace-root>/.claude/`" — which is exactly the state this release ended in. `gh pr merge --squash --delete-branch` reported only a local-branch deletion failure (a live sibling worktree held the ref); the merge itself was established by protocol rather than by the command's output — `state == "MERGED"` and `mergeCommit.oid` non-null, with `git rev-parse origin/main` independently returning `63dcbe8e` from the remote. Readying the PR did **not** expand the check population on this repo: 48 check-runs before `gh pr ready`, **48** after, all SUCCESS — the documented 48→67 expansion did not reproduce here, and the number was re-polled rather than carried. Signed-annotated tag `v4.33` bound at the Stage-12 atomic ref-CAS via `claim-version.sh --bump minor --stamp-slug operator-runtime-state-and-workspace-anchoring --stamp-branch chore/v4.33-stage-12-release-log`, first-attempt CAS win off anchor v4.32. **The tool halted a first invocation rather than proceeding**: HEAD was `main`, and the stamp commit would have pushed directly to the mainline that `git-workflow.md` forbids; the correctly-named chore branch was created and the claim re-run. The claim-time stamp was verified by observation on the pushed remote, not inferred from exit 0 — the precise failure v4.31 shipped: `plans/v4/v4.33_RELEASE_PLAN.md` exists and is git-tracked, the flat slug-named plan returns **0** rows from `git ls-files`, **0** `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}` tokens remain against a sensitivity arm counting **1** literal `v4.33` in the same file, `git cat-file -t v4.33` returns `tag` (a real tag object, not a lightweight ref) with a **Good ED25519 signature**, and `refs/tags/v4.33^{}` read from `git ls-remote` dereferences to `63dcbe8e3fd9c3985d7a603f0d700e2f155a66a5` — tag↔SHA identity **MATCH**, read from the remote rather than the local copy, with the tagger line carrying the GitHub no-reply address.
**Timestamp:** 2026-08-20 18:16 (merge event, UTC)
**Cycle-Time:** N/A — (T_GO=absent; T_DEPLOY=absent; mechanism: compute-cycle-time.sh). **Both key forms were run rather than one**, per the v4.26–v4.32 precedent, and both return N/A — but the cause here is this run's own emission discipline rather than a content-only release. The Stage-9 GO was recorded as `decision`/`d-class` rather than `gate-outcome`/`plan-review-go`, so the T_GO anchor the tool seeks was never written under the shape it queries; and no `deployment-status/deploy-skill` or `deploy-harness` row was emitted, because the live deploy propagated hooks and libraries rather than a skill or harness target. Recorded as the governed tool returns it, with the reason named so it reads as a known gap rather than an unexplained blank.
**Velocity:** planned 26 pts / delivered 26 pts (1.00); files-changed 56; allocation 0/26/0 pts (feature/debt/protocol-slack); class cross-cutting; mechanism: compute-release-velocity.sh. `[SOURCE]` **Delivered and allocation corrected under #4927** — the original `4 pts (0.15)` was read from member CLOSE STATE at Stage-13 authoring time, before the close-out transitioned the bundle's issues, so it counted only what happened to be closed already. Recomputed over the same membership on the close-state-independent delivery predicate; `planned` re-verified unchanged against the recorded value, and this milestone has no Phase-A2-demilestoned members to recover.
**Result:** SUCCESS — release PR #5632 merged to main; `v4.33` signed-annotated tag pushed at the merge SHA and verified from the origin ref; the claim-time stamp fired and was verified against the pushed remote. The live deploy ran from this session via `./update.sh` and **partially landed**: the co-deploy placed `command-position.awk` into the deployed hook library for the first time (5 → 6 files under `hooks/lib/`), `assert_hook_lib_closure` reported **PASS (4 required libraries present)** read from its own line rather than inferred from exit status, and the durable hook-bundle snapshot introduced by this release **fired on a real deploy** (0 → 1 generation, `20260820T181842Z-74588`) — the first exercise of #5662/#5669 outside a fixture. The regenerated `script-execution-allowlist.txt` retained its `test_refresh_hooks.sh` rows through a tamper-detected rebuild, confirming the source-side fix rather than the host-local one. **Outstanding at close, recorded rather than silently carried:** (i) **the operator's hook-bundle deploy is incomplete and operator-owed** — the refresh classified **all four** release-modified security hooks as `PRESERVED (operator-edited)` and did not overwrite them, and that classification is a **100% false positive**: each deployed hook matches a historical source commit exactly (`block-egress.sh`, `block-fs-boundary.sh`, `block-rm-prefer-trash.sh` ← `6dadf334` 2026-07-25; `block-destructive.sh` ← `81e35588` 2026-08-10), so they are stale platform copies rather than local edits and nothing operator-authored would be lost by reconciling. **12 of 22** deployed hooks differ from source at close (10 match, 0 missing), including all four the release changed; the remedy the installer itself prints is a `setup-workspace.sh` re-run, which is agent-blocked by `BLOCK-DESTRUCTIVE-022` and was handed to the operator as a terminal command rather than bypassed. (ii) **A passing closure assertion does not imply the release deployed** — `assert_hook_lib_closure` derives its required set *from the deployed hooks*, so the old bundle's 4 libraries satisfy it and it would have passed identically had the release never existed; the incompleteness was surfaced only by hash-comparing deployed against source, which is why the Stage-12 verification is recorded by hash rather than by the three green signals (`exit 0`, closure PASS, "Update complete") that were each individually accurate. (iii) the `core/rules/` × 4 mirror reaches no runtime because the mirror has **no producer** — pre-existing and separately owned by #633, with this release's instance recorded there rather than re-filed. (iv) the release branch is retained rather than deleted, a live sibling worktree holding the ref.
**Outcome:** SUCCESS

#### Deployment Log v4.32
**Files deployed:** `plan-file-identity-adr-092` shipped as one release PR #5581 (`release/plan-file-identity-adr-092 → main`, merge commit `d9bf8517`), 6 delivery slices across 30 commits on one branch, **50 files (+4021/−119)**. The denominator was read three-dot from the pre-merge mainline `eaf93963...c0d3bea5`; the Phase A.5.3 forward-merge made the branch a strict superset of `main` (`git merge-base` returns `eaf93963`, i.e. the pre-merge mainline tip itself), so the two-dot and three-dot forms cannot disagree here — the divergence class recorded at v4.27 is structurally absent rather than merely unobserved. **The Stage-12 spoke could not be spawned, and the tail ran hub-direct under the framework's own recovery fork.** The `Agent`-tool spawn of the `pmo-release-manager` tail was refused by the session's command classifier. Rather than stall an authorized gate, B1/B3/B5 were executed hub-direct — the `delegation` fork the orchestration playbook defines, with `merit:recovery`. The property the spawn mandate protects is that the merge is never a bare `gh pr merge` shorn of its version claim and DEPLOYED row; all three steps ran, in order, each verified against remote state rather than command exit. The companion `gate-outcome/goal-conformance` telemetry row for Stage 9 (verdict ALIGNED, 4/4 Outcome clauses met) was **refused by the same classifier twice** and carried as owed, then **succeeded on a later retry** — so the refusal is nondeterministic rather than a standing policy, and the row is written. Recorded because the two failure modes have opposite remediations: a standing refusal needs a permission change, an intermittent one needs a retry. The distinguishing evidence is that three `append-pipeline-event.sh` invocations with the same shape produced two refusals and two successes across the run.

The trigger was real rather than hypothetical: `mergeStateStatus` read **`CLEAN`** at the Stage-9 gate and at the pre-merge re-check, then flipped to **`BLOCKED`** the instant the PR was marked ready-for-review, because that transition re-triggered **7** check-runs (2 of them required contexts) into `IN_PROGRESS`. This is precisely the state A.6.2a exists for — `BLOCKED` with zero *failing* required contexts, visually indistinguishable from a broken build, with `--admin` as the reflex. The governed query was run: `select(.commit.verification.verified == false)` over the PR's commits returned **no rows**, against a control arm counting the whole population — **9 commits, 9 `verified: true`, 0 `false`** — so the zero discriminates rather than reading an empty page. **`BLOCKED` cause = not signature-related**; the cause was the re-triggered checks, which resolved to `46 pass / 0 non-pass` and returned the PR to `CLEAN`, at which point the plain merge succeeded. Passing the flag would have refused the claim outright. A second, independent reason applies here: `_host_commit_push` pushes `HEAD`, and this claim ran from a session worktree whose HEAD is not `main`, so the flag would have pushed a worktree branch to origin. A non-zero *behind* count with a zero intersection is benign; chasing `main` here would not have converged, since two siblings remain in flight.

**The version was claimed under live four-way contention and won its slot on the first attempt.** Four sibling release PRs were open against the same anchor `v4.24` when this stage entered, three of them `minor` off that same anchor, so all four recomputed to the same next-free slot. The A.5.6a pre-merge dry-run resolved **`v4.25`** free (control arm: the same `ls-remote` query returns a ref line for `v4.24`, so the empty result is a real absence and not a dead probe), and the Phase B3 ref-CAS then won `v4.25` without a single collision retry. **There was no provisional to correct:** this is an ADR-092 slug-primary release carrying no version literal anywhere — not in the branch name (`release/reference-constant-integrity`), not in the plan filename, and not in the PR title. Measured on the plan body, `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}` × **2** and the literal `v4.25` × **0** pre-claim (sensitivity control: `v4.24`, the anchor, × **2** in the same file). The A.5.6b/c comparison is therefore vacuous by construction rather than skipped, and the claim sequence closes at a single hop — **`v4.25`**, no re-version ledger row owed.

**The tag message deliberately carries no version digit, and that is the plan's own invariant rather than an omission.** `claim-version.sh` builds its default message post-CAS from the won tag, but a `--message` supplied at invocation is authored *pre*-CAS — so spelling a digit into it would have meant guessing the slot before the compare-and-swap arbitrated it, which the plan's Invariant 1 (*"no durable artifact may hardcode a concrete version before the Stage-12 atomic claim"*) forbids, in exactly the drift class this milestone exists to fix. The message therefore carries the audit triplet (slug, issue count, originating PR) and lets the tag's own name supply the version. Had the CAS bumped, the message would still have been correct.

**The six delivery issues auto-closed at the merge.** PR #5508's body carried `closingIssuesReferences` = **6** (#4459, #4460, #4461, #4465, #4900, #4902), so GitHub closed all six at `mergedAt`. Stage 13 inherits them already closed and should **verify** closure rather than perform it.

**The ADR-092 claim-time stamp ran through the tool rather than by hand, and landed on this chore branch by design.** `_stamp_release_identity` pushes its follow-on commit with `git push origin HEAD`, so the branch checked out at claim time is the branch the stamp lands on. This stage therefore created `chore/v4.25-stage-12-release-log` **at the merge SHA before invoking the claim**, so the stamp commit (`0092e5e5`) rides this same chore PR to `main` instead of being pushed to a session ref or requiring a prohibited direct-to-main commit. Post-stamp the plan reads `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}` × **0** (was 2) and `v4.25` × **2** (was 0), and `release/releases/plans/reference-constant-integrity_RELEASE_PLAN.md` is renamed to `release/releases/plans/v4/v4.25_RELEASE_PLAN.md`. This is the first release in this lineage to complete the stamp pass in-tool: v4.23 HALTed its pre-flight (zero tokens) and v4.24 omitted the flag because its `HEAD` was neither `main` nor the release branch — the branch-at-merge-SHA ordering used here resolves that constraint rather than working around it.

The three bucket counts sum to **40**, matching the diff denominator exactly, so the census is complete rather than a sample that happened to find nothing. Against that population `(core|operations|release)/skills/` returns × **0** — control: `core/` returns × **32** on the same census, so the zero discriminates rather than reading a dead filter — and `packages/` returns × **0** (controls: `.github/` × **3**, `release/` × **5**). **Mechanism:** git merge-commit (PR #5257 → main at `84245e6681a0a533e1611abddab4e59d13bc245c`; a true **two-parent** merge commit — parents `1e960932` (main) and `7f664e0c` (release head), confirmed by `git rev-list --parents -n 1` returning three SHAs, and independently by the REST commit object reporting the same two parents — so the plan's declared `git revert -m 1` first-parent rollback form applies and is intact, and the squash that would have collapsed 40 commits into one parent was structurally forbidden rather than merely discouraged). **The plain `gh pr merge 5257 --merge` succeeded; `--admin` was neither used nor needed** — the fifth consecutive release to land the plain merge under this branch-protection configuration (`enforce_admins:false`, 0 required reviews, `required_linear_history:false`). **`gh pr merge` again returned exit 0 with empty output** — the tenth consecutive release to reproduce the silence the Phase B merge verification protocol exists for — so the merge was established by that protocol and not by the command's quiet: `state == "MERGED"` **and** `mergeCommit.oid` non-null, both asserted post-hoc against the GraphQL and REST views independently, merged at `2026-08-12T23:33:21Z`. Checks were read as **parsed rows, not exit status**, paginated at `per_page=100`: **46 check-runs, 46 `completed`/`success`, 0 non-passing**, and the required set measured against a **proven denominator of 9** — all 9 required contexts present by name and passing, none missing. (The combined-status API reads `state: pending` with **0** statuses; that is the no-legacy-commit-statuses case, not a failure — every required context on this repo is a check-run.) Zero force pushes, zero rebases, zero history rewrites **in this stage**, zero signature bypasses, no `--no-verify`, no push to `main`. Signed-annotated tag `v4.24` at the merge SHA (annotated tag object `d7602cb4d20f31c503eadd21ad44f31b82b0cf73`; `git cat-file -t v4.24` → `tag`, not `commit`, i.e. a real tag object and not a lightweight ref; **signature verified via the GitHub API** — `repos/.../git/tags/d7602cb4` reports `verified: true, reason: valid` — rather than via local `%G?`, which is unreliable on this machine; `v4.24` dereferences to `84245e6681a0a533e1611abddab4e59d13bc245c` — tag↔SHA identity **MATCH**; the tagger line carries the GitHub no-reply address, read from the tag object rather than inferred). The merge commit itself is likewise API-verified (`verified: true, reason: valid`), as `required_signatures: true` on `main` demands. **This release's branch was re-signed before it could merge, and that is materially unusual enough to belong in the deployment record rather than only in the sub-task thread.** The first Stage-12 attempt **HALTed at Phase B1**: the plain merge was rejected with *"the base branch policy prohibits the merge"*, because `main` carries `required_signatures: true` and **12 of the branch's 40 commits were unsigned** (GitHub reported **12 `unsigned` / 28 `valid`**). Every other protection dimension was already satisfied, so signatures were the sole unmet policy. `--admin` was **not** reached for — on a genuine signature-policy block it would have been a real signing bypass, which the Stage-12 never-bypass-signing constraint forbids — and B3/B5 were deliberately not started, so the halt mutated nothing. The operator then **explicitly authorized a history rewrite**, which re-signed the branch: tip `168cbe02` → **`7f664e0c`**, tally **28 `G` / 12 `N` → 40 `G` / 0 `N`**, **35 of 40 SHAs changed** while 5 round-tripped to identical SHAs, pushed with `--force-with-lease`. **The rewrite changed signatures and nothing else** — the tip tree object is byte-identical to the pre-rewrite tree, so no citation's *meaning* changed, only its address. CI re-ran **46/46 pass (9/9 required)** and PR #5257 moved `BLOCKED` → **`CLEAN`**. This retry re-verified the end state independently before merging: the compare API reports **verified 40 / unverified 0** across `main...release/triage-and-backlog-instrumentation`. No branch protection was relaxed at any point — `required_signatures: true` is unchanged on `main`. **Audit-trail residue, stated rather than smoothed over:** the 35 superseded SHAs no longer exist on the branch, so any earlier record citing them resolves only through the mapping table in the re-signing comment on the Stage-12 sub-task. Residue class **R-2**, disposition *accept as residual with documented provenance*; this paragraph is that provenance. The rewrite authorization was consumed by the re-signing and did **not** extend to this stage, which performed no force push, rebase, or amend.

**The seven delivery issues auto-closed at the merge, which is a departure from the two preceding releases and is recorded as fact rather than as intent.** PR #5257's body carried `closingIssuesReferences` = **7** (#4462, #4463, #4708, #4891, #4899, #4901, #4926), so GitHub closed all seven within six seconds of the merge (`2026-08-12T23:33:22Z`–`23:33:27Z` against `mergedAt 2026-08-12T23:33:21Z`). v4.22 and v4.23 were each authored parser-clean (`closingIssuesReferences` = 0) so that Stage 13 owned the close; here Stage 13 inherits them **already closed**. Nothing was lost — the issues are genuinely delivered — but Stage 13 should verify closure rather than perform it.

**The claim-time stamp was carried in this chore PR rather than by `claim-version.sh --stamp-slug`, deliberately.** The plan carries **3** `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}` tokens, so the stamp pass was live rather than vacuous — but `_stamp_release_identity` pushes its follow-on commit with `git push origin HEAD`, and Stage 12 ran from a worktree whose `HEAD` was neither `main` nor the release branch, so passing the flag would have pushed the stamp to the wrong ref (and the release branch was auto-deleted at the merge in any case). The flag was therefore omitted, the CAS ran clean without it, and the substitution plus the `git mv` into `release/releases/plans/v4/v4.24_RELEASE_PLAN.md` land here instead — replicating the tool's own blind global `sed` byte-for-byte so the artifact stays reproducible. **Residue from that fidelity:** two of the three substitutions sit in prose that describes the token itself, which now reads "carries the literal token `v4.24`". That is exactly what the tool would have produced; reconciling the wording is owed to Stage 13, and is recorded here rather than silently improved.

**The provisional died before the merge, and A.5.6 is why that cost a re-read instead of a stale label on an immutable commit.** The branch carried `v4.22` as its recorded determination; the A.5.6a dry-run recomputed next-free as **`v4.23`**, because the in-flight sibling `closeout-output-completeness` — which had recorded the *same* provisional at its own Commit 0 against the same baseline — reached the claim first and has already closed out to `VERIFIED`. That is the `A.5.6c` DIFFER arm, whose named remediation is a pre-merge relabel. **Here that remediation is vacuous, and it was verified so rather than waved past:** this is an ADR-092 slug-primary release carrying **no version literal to correct** — not in the branch name, not in the plan filename, not in the PR title, and not in any frontmatter. A full-corpus scan of all 35 commit messages (68,617 bytes extracted) found exactly **2** version occurrences, both inside Commit 0's re-verify paragraph, which states its own provisionality: *"that is arbitrated by the Stage-12 atomic compare-and-swap, and no version literal ships in any artifact this release produces."* Manufacturing a "corrected label" would have meant *adding* a version literal to a release that deliberately ships none. The Phase B3 CAS then won `v4.23` on its first attempt, so the claim sequence closes at **`v4.22 → v4.23`** with Stage 12 contributing no further hop. **Residue, stated rather than smoothed over:** the plan's Header row and Commit 0's message both still read `v4.22`. The Header row is reconcilable and is owed at Stage 13 with the plan rename; the commit message is immutable without a force-push and stays as an honest as-authored artifact — residue class **R-2**, disposition *accept as residual with documented provenance*, and this paragraph is that provenance.

**No orphan tag was left, so `RELEASE_REVERSIONS.md` gets no row.** `v4.22` was a **provisional name only** — this release never pushed a tag for it, and it is the live canonical tag of the sibling that won it (`v4.22` → `closeout-output-completeness`). Under the ledger's own rule that is `disposition = none`, and the runbook records a row *only* when a tag was actually pushed and is canonical for nothing. Phase B5.8 is therefore **N/A by rule, not by omission**.

**The stamp pre-flight was attempted first, HALTed, and the HALT was verified to have claimed nothing.** `--stamp-slug` was passed deliberately rather than reasoned around: the pre-flight is **pre-CAS and mutates nothing** (STEP 2.7, ahead of STEP 3's push), so attempting it strictly dominates predicting its outcome. It refused with *"plan … carries no `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}` token to resolve; HALT — not claiming"* — this plan carries **0** tokens, measured, against a control pattern returning **3** in the same file. That the script *says* it claimed nothing is not evidence, so the abort was checked on four arms against a pinned pre-state: local tags unchanged at `v4.20 v4.21 v4.22` with no `v4.23`; `v4.23` absent from origin (control: the same query returns a ref line for `v4.22`, so the empty result is a real absence); working tree clean; `HEAD` unchanged at `fd763a1c`. The claim was then re-run without the flag. **Gate Check 59 is window-gated on the token's presence and SKIPs cleanly — no gate was bypassed and no step was dropped.** The consequence is that the plan file did **not** auto-rename, and the `git mv` into `release/releases/plans/v4/v4.23_RELEASE_PLAN.md` is owed at Stage 13 rather than lost.

**Cycle-Time is N/A on the deploy anchor, and the reason was measured rather than assumed.** The first invocation keyed by `v4.23` reported *both* anchors missing; keyed by the milestone slug — the event log's § 2a release join key — the gate anchor resolves to `2026-08-12T19:04:49Z` and only T_DEPLOY is genuinely absent. The version-keyed form does not yet resolve because the version↔slug binding it reads is *this row*, which did not exist when the tool ran; it is expected to resolve once this row lands. Recording the distinction because an unexamined `N/A` on this field has already had to be reconciled backwards once on an earlier release, and the difference between "no anchor exists" and "the query used the wrong key" is invisible in the emitted value.

**The version slot held, and A.5.6 is why that is a measurement rather than an assumption.** This release had already been re-versioned **twice** — `v4.20 → v4.21 → v4.22` — each time because a sibling claimed the slot mid-flight, so a third loss was the expected case, not the tail. The A.5.6 pre-merge dry-run against `origin/main` recomputed next-free as **`v4.22`**, matching the carried label: the `A.5.6b` FREE arm, `version-freeness = FREE`. The Phase B3 CAS then won the slot on its first attempt with no collision retry, so the claim sequence closes at **`v4.20 → v4.21 → v4.22`** and Stage 12 contributed no further hop.

**No orphan tag was left by either prior slot loss, so `RELEASE_REVERSIONS.md` gets no row.** `v4.20` and `v4.21` were **provisional names only** — this release never pushed a tag for either, and each is the live canonical tag of the sibling that won it (`v4.20` → `hook-precision-and-boundaries`, `v4.21` → `ci-wiring-and-flake-elimination`). Under the ledger's own rule that is `disposition = none`, and the runbook records a row *only* when a tag was actually pushed and is canonical for nothing. Phase B5.8 is therefore **N/A by rule, not by omission**.

**Cycle-Time is `N/A` because the anchor is genuinely absent, and the field says so rather than inventing a number.** `compute-cycle-time.sh` resolves `T_GO=2026-08-10T17:37:44Z` but finds no `deployment-status/deploy-skill` or `deploy-harness` event for the slug — correct, since this release deploys no skill and no harness artifact (see the zero-skill census above). The value is owed a Stage-13 reconcile only if a deploy event lands there; absent one, `N/A` is the terminal honest value, not a placeholder.

**F-02 composition-surface refresh: delivered, and it surfaced a pre-existing token defect.** The `compute-close-class-telemetry.sh` + `test_close_class_telemetry.sh` allowlist rows land on `main` in this merge but reach the *deployed* surface only via `update.sh --surfaces-only`, which therefore had to run post-merge. Verified **by content, not by exit status**: a structural probe over the whole deployed allowlist returned **0 → 10** rows (2 entries × 5 path forms), against a control entry that held steady at 4, so the refresh discriminates in both directions. That content check then caught what an exit-status check would have passed: `[PMO_PLATFORM_ROOT]` had resolved to the *running worktree's* path, and the pre-refresh backup showed the deployed surfaces had **already** been carrying a stale worktree path from an earlier release's spoke. Root cause is in `core/deploy/compose.py`, which resolves that token by precedence `--repo-root` > `$PMO_PLATFORM_ROOT` > *compose.py self-location* — so any `update.sh` run from a worktree silently bakes the worktree path in, and release work routinely runs in worktrees. Repaired in-stage by re-running with the token pinned to the repo root plus `--force-regen` (needed because an unchanged source SHA would otherwise have reported "unchanged" and left the bad value in place); all 20 surfaces regenerated with OPERATOR-ADDITIONS fences preserved verbatim per the ADR-014 contract. Functional impact was low throughout — the `*/release/tools/…` glob form matches any absolute path, so the allowlists kept working — making this a correctness-and-drift defect rather than an outage. **The durable fix is not in this release** and is owed a separate intake item.

**Files deployed:** **two skills took the S-2 copy in this stage; one `.skill` package is measurably stale on `main` and is owed to Stage 13.** `ci-wiring-and-flake-elimination` shipped as one release PR #5085 (`release/ci-wiring-and-flake-elimination → main`, merge commit `6edfc44d`), 3 delivery slices (#4188, #3832, #3936) on one branch, **51 files (+2,199/−386)** — read from the merge commit's first-parent diff (`7d7cbcec..6edfc44d`). Only the first is this release's to fix; it is rebuilt in the Stage-13 chore PR under Phase B5.9, which is where the codification places it.
**Mechanism:** git merge-commit (PR #5085 → main at `6edfc44d916735f818ff5e0aa69ce38070f39029`; a true **two-parent** merge commit — parents `7d7cbcec` (main) and `fa435b3b` (release head), confirmed by `git rev-list --parents -n 1` returning three SHAs — so the plan's declared revert-the-merge rollback form applies and is intact). **The plain `gh pr merge 5085 --merge` succeeded; `--admin` was neither used nor needed.** That is the second consecutive release to attempt the plain merge under this branch-protection configuration and land it, which is now enough to say the standing "release merges require `--admin`" guidance is not borne out at N=2 attempts. **`gh pr merge` returned exit 0 with neither stdout nor stderr** — the seventh consecutive release to reproduce the empirical motivation the Phase B merge verification protocol was codified from — and the merge was established by that protocol rather than by the command's silence: `state == "MERGED"` **and** `mergeCommit.oid` non-null. Zero force pushes, zero rebases, zero history rewrites, zero signature bypasses, no `--no-verify`. Signed-annotated tag `v4.21` at the merge SHA (annotated tag object `685fe4ef71a4082dd131d6c27aaeecc0ca5ff02a`; `git cat-file -t v4.21` → `tag`, not `commit`, i.e. a tag object and not a lightweight ref; `git tag -v v4.21` reports **`Good "git" signature`** with the ED25519/SSH key; `v4.21` dereferences to `6edfc44d916735f818ff5e0aa69ce38070f39029` — tag↔SHA identity **MATCH**). Claim bound at the Stage-12 **atomic ref-CAS** via `claim-version.sh --sha 6edfc44d9167… --bump minor` — **first-attempt CAS win, zero collisions, zero signing bypasses**; bump-class `minor`. **`--stamp-slug` was deliberately NOT passed**: the plan carries **0** `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}` tokens (it bakes a literal provisional version instead), so the ADR-092 stamp pre-flight would have refused with *"plan carries no `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}` token to resolve"* — and per ADR-092 the stamping pass is skipped entirely absent the flag, which is the correct shape for a pre-token plan rather than a dropped step.

**Cycle-Time:** 25m — (T_GO=2026-08-09T15:27:09Z; T_DEPLOY=2026-08-09T15:52:32Z; mechanism: compute-cycle-time.sh)

**Result:** SUCCESS — release PR #5085 merged to main via a true two-parent merge commit; `v4.21` signed-annotated tag pushed at the merge SHA and verified; all three release issues auto-closed by the PR's Issue References block; both changed skills S-2-deployed. **One item is owed forward rather than silently dropped:** the `finops-usage-extractor` `.skill` rebuild, to Stage 13 Phase B5.9.
**Outcome:** SUCCESS

**The version slot moved for the sixth time, and A.5.6 caught it before the merge rather than after.** The branch carried `v4.19` as its provisional label; `claim-version.sh --dry-run` at Phase A.5.6a recomputed next-free as **`v4.21`** — `v4.19` and `v4.20` had both been claimed by siblings since this branch's last re-verify. That is the `A.5.6c` DIFFER arm, and its purpose is to obtain the operator's re-version disposition before the merge lands a stale label; the disposition was **already rendered pre-flight** — the Stage-12 Execute authorization names `v4.21` as the planned version and states it is provisional-until-CAS. The pre-merge relabel that `A.5.6c` also names was **not** applied to the branch, and the reason is recorded rather than assumed: this plan is a **pre-ADR-092-token** plan that bakes a literal version, so relabelling it means a content edit plus a full CI re-run — another multi-minute window for a seventh slot loss on a release that has already lost five. ADR-092 moves identity binding to the **CAS-win path** for exactly this reason, so the merge went first and the number was won at the CAS. The consequence is honest and bounded: the merged commit messages, the PR title and the plan body carry `v4.19` as an immutable as-authored artifact — residue class **R-2** in the re-version-recovery doctrine, whose fixed disposition is *accept as residual with documented provenance*. This paragraph is that provenance. The **R-3** corpus roll-forward (this row, the tag, INDEX / DIGEST / NOTES / CHANGELOG / `.version`) all carry `v4.21`.

**No orphan tag was left by any of the five prior slot losses, so `RELEASE_REVERSIONS.md` gets no row.** The abandoned versions `v4.16`–`v4.20` were **provisional names only** — this release never pushed a tag for any of them, and each is the live canonical tag of the sibling that won it (`v4.16` → `methodology-fields-and-statuses`, `v4.17` → `102-specialist-role-coverage`, `v4.18` → `hub-spoke-execution-safety`, `v4.19` → `58-task-artifact-lifecycle-and-knowledge`, `v4.20` → `hook-precision-and-boundaries`). Under the ledger's own rule that is `disposition = none`, and the runbook is explicit that a row is recorded *only* when a tag was actually pushed and is canonical for nothing. Phase B5.8 is therefore **N/A by rule, not by omission**.

**A.5.7 did not fire, and the omission is the correct signal.** The branch adds **0** `ADR-NNN-*.md` files (`git diff --name-only origin/main...origin/release/… | grep -E 'ADR-[0-9]{3}.*\.md$'` → no match), so the ADR-number freeness sub-step has nothing to check. Gate `G-EX9` does not fire for this stage.

**A.5 file-dimension probe: clean, and the divergence was corpus-only.** `origin/main` stood **5** commits ahead of the release head at entry. The A.5.3 merge probe was executed in the session worktree — `Merge made by the 'ort' strategy`, **zero conflicts**, probe merge `c1867375` — and was *not* pushed, per A.5.3's own wording (the step merges in the session worktree; it does not re-baseline the branch). The 5 commits touch only release-corpus ledgers (`.version`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `RELEASE_DIGEST.md`, `RELEASE_INDEX.md`, `RELEASE_LOG.md`, `notes/v4.20_RELEASE_NOTES.md`, and the v4.20 plan rename) — **zero** intersection with this release's 51-file set. That also settles the PR's own Known-item 4: Arm D's `main`-side denominator cannot have moved on a corpus-only divergence, so no re-grade is owed.

**Phase A.4 skipped by rule.** The release plan declares no merge strategy (Delivery Strategy records topology `D-C SINGLE` / concurrency `P0` / one PR / one merge, and names no `merge`/`squash`/`rebase`), so A.4.1 routes straight past A.4.2–A.4.5 and the default `gh pr merge --merge` semantics apply. **Phase B0 was a measured no-op** — `gh pr list --base release/ci-wiring-and-flake-elimination --state open` returned **0** dependents, so no base-shift was needed and `--delete-branch` was correctly omitted (branch removal is Phase C4's, and a live worktree still holds the branch).

**Mechanism:** git merge-commit (PR #5081 → main at `e74cc06f330a8938596612d74aa5b03aa8db81df`; a true **two-parent** merge commit — parents `ef15df92` (main) and `cc57cb78` (release head), confirmed by `git rev-list --parents -n 1` returning three SHAs — so the plan's declared revert-the-merge rollback form applies and is intact). **The plain `gh pr merge 5081 --merge` succeeded; `--admin` was neither used nor needed.** That is a directly-informative datapoint against the standing "release merges require `--admin`" guidance: the immediately-preceding release recorded that it used `--admin` but explicitly declined to claim it was *required*, because no plain merge had been attempted. One was attempted here, under the same branch-protection configuration, and it merged. **`gh pr merge` returned exit 0 with neither stdout nor stderr** — the sixth consecutive release to reproduce the empirical motivation the Phase B merge verification protocol was codified from — and the merge was established by that protocol rather than by the command's silence: `state == "MERGED"` **and** `mergeCommit.oid` non-null, merged at `2026-08-09T04:06:20Z`. Claim bound at the Stage-12 **atomic ref-CAS** via `claim-version.sh --sha e74cc06f330a… --bump minor --stamp-slug hook-precision-and-boundaries` — **first-attempt CAS win, zero collisions, zero signing bypasses**; bump-class `minor`.

**Result:** SUCCESS — release PR #5081 merged to main via a true two-parent merge commit; `v4.20` signed-annotated tag pushed at the merge SHA and verified; claim-time stamp landed with zero token residue. **One item is owed forward rather than silently dropped:** the Phase B4 S-2 copy of `release-hub` to the installed skill path.
**Outcome:** PARTIAL
**Outcome rationale:** 2 of 3 Outcome-Statement limbs delivered; limb 2 (security-reminder matcher precision) was mis-stated at bundling and never in this release's scope — Deviation Log E-16

**The stamp pre-flight HALTed once, fail-closed, and the retry is the mechanism working rather than a workaround.** The first `claim-version.sh` invocation exited non-zero with `stamp pre-flight — no pre-claim plan …; HALT — not claiming`: the session worktree still stood at the pre-merge main tip, so the slug-primary plan file did not yet exist locally for the stamping pass to read. **No tag was pushed** — `git tag -l 'v4.20'` returned empty after the HALT, confirming the abort was clean rather than partial. The worktree was fast-forwarded to the merge commit and the claim re-run, winning `v4.20` on its first CAS attempt. The lesson is narrow and worth carrying: the stamp pre-flight reads the **local tree**, so a Stage-12 spoke must advance its worktree to the merge commit *before* invoking the claim.

**`--stamp-slug` ran and is verified by residue, not by exit code.** `{{RELEASE_VERSION}} → v4.20` resolved with **0** unresolved tokens remaining in `release/releases/plans/v4/v4.20_RELEASE_PLAN.md`, against a **sensitivity arm of 2** pre-claim tokens counted on the merge commit and **2** resolved `v4.20` occurrences in the stamped file — the two figures match exactly, so every substitution site is accounted for and the zero is not a read of the wrong file. The residue probe counted *occurrences* (`grep -o … | wc -l`) rather than lines, because two tokens could have shared one line. The plan renamed from `release/releases/plans/hook-precision-and-boundaries_RELEASE_PLAN.md` to `release/releases/plans/v4/v4.20_RELEASE_PLAN.md` (old path confirmed absent), in stamp commit `ade87a0e` riding this PR.

**The stamp reaches `main` through this PR rather than around it — but by repair, not by construction.** The claim was invoked from the session worktree's own branch rather than from a pre-created chore branch, so `_host_commit_push` pushed the stamp commit to `refs/heads/worktree-agent-a69c8a27911dcd817` on origin instead of to a chore branch. **`origin/main` was never written** — it stood at `e74cc06f` (the merge, unchanged) throughout, verified directly rather than assumed. The chore branch was then cut from the stamp commit, so the stamp rides this PR's review exactly as intended; the stray session branch is recorded here for deletion at close-out. The v4.15/v4.18/v4.19 precedent — create and push the chore branch *before* invoking the claim — is the shape that avoids the repair, and this release did not follow it.

**A.5.6 had no carried version to compare, and that is the ADR-092 mechanism working as designed.** The plan is slug-primary and carries `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}` rather than a baked digit, so the `A.5.6b/c` carried-vs-recomputed comparison has no operand — the release holds no slot a sibling could take. That matters here more than most: this milestone had its version claimed out from under it **four times** (`v4.15`, `v4.16`, `v4.17`, `v4.18`) under the older shape. Freeness was instead established directly — the adapter dry-run computed next-free `v4.20` both before the merge (against tip `ef15df92`) and again after it (against tip `e74cc06f`), corroborated by an independent recomputation from tags (max `v4.19`) and from the ledger (max `v4.19`), with a two-arm probe returning `v4.19` × **1** (sensitivity) and `v4.20` × **0** (specificity) — and the CAS then won `v4.20` on the first attempt.

**A.5.7 returned BINDS and no renumber was required, because the Stage-8 renumber had already reconciled it.** This branch adds exactly one ADR, `core/ADRs/ADR-130-lib-missing-guard-is-mode-coupled.md`. Mainline's anchor is **ADR-129** — taken by the immediately-preceding release, whose own log recorded this branch as the later claimant per **ADR-115** — so 130 is both free and contiguous; `ADR-130` × **0** on `origin/main` across **both** `core/ADRs/` and `release/ADRs/`, against a specificity control of `ADR-129` × **1** on the identical probe, so the zero discriminates. Deviation-Log entry **E-14** records the branch-side `ADR-129 → ADR-130` move that made this a BINDS. Gate `G-EX9` does not fire for this stage.

**Release-issue auto-close did not fire, by design rather than by defect.** #4186, #3805 and #3429 all read `OPEN` immediately post-merge. The release PR body carries no close-family keyword — it uses safe cross-reference phrasing throughout, per the parser-clean PR-body discipline — so closure is Stage 13's to perform at close-out. Recorded here so a later reader does not mistake three open issues under a `DEPLOYED` row for a missed Phase B1 step.

**One post-deploy operator action is outstanding, and the release is not fully realised without it.** The hook-wiring re-home extends hook loading to repo- and worktree-rooted sessions; until it runs on a given instance the path-leak guard is inactive there at any mode setting, and the warn log it would populate stays empty in a way indistinguishable from clean. Plan risk **R8** and Deviation-Log entries **D-A/D-G** carry it as a tracked action with a verification, not a deferral. Read the install validator's hook-wiring re-home verdict before reading any warn-log result.

**`--stamp-slug` ran and is verified by residue, not by exit code — this is the AI-006 discharge.** The flag whose omission is *silent* (absent it the stamping pass is skipped entirely and behavior is byte-identical to the pre-stamp script) was passed, and the result was measured rather than inferred from the script's clean exit. `{{RELEASE_VERSION}} → v4.19` resolved with **0** unresolved tokens remaining in `release/releases/plans/v4/v4.19_RELEASE_PLAN.md`, against a **sensitivity arm of 3** resolved `v4.19` occurrences in that same file — a figure that matches the **3** pre-claim tokens counted on the release branch exactly, so every substitution site is accounted for and the zero is not a read of the wrong file. Verified post-hoc rather than assumed: `origin/main` stood at `5ffa57c2` (the merge, unchanged) while `origin/chore/v4.19-stage-12-release-log` stood at `914226a4` (the stamp) — so the stamp reaches `main` through this PR's review, not around it.

**A.5.6 had no carried version to compare, and that is the ADR-092 mechanism working as designed.** The plan is slug-primary and carries `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}`, not a baked digit, so the `A.5.6b/c` carried-vs-recomputed comparison has no operand — the release holds no slot a sibling could take. Freeness was instead established directly by the adapter dry-run, which computed next-free `v4.19` both before the merge (against tip `a5cd7601`) and again after it (against tip `5ffa57c2`), and the CAS then won `v4.19` on the first attempt. The plan's own R7 risk row called this outcome in advance: two prior D-Version determinations (`v4.15`, then `v4.16`) were each lost to a concurrent release, and the fix was to change the shape rather than the digit.

**A.5.7 fired, returned BINDS, and no renumber was required — the Stage-9 determination held.** This branch adds exactly one ADR, `release/ADRs/ADR-129-close-class-is-a-declared-deliverable-value-conditioning-one-gate-spec.md`. Mainline's anchor was **ADR-128**, so 129 is both free and contiguous; `ADR-129` × **0** on `origin/main` across **both** `core/ADRs/` and `release/ADRs/`, against a specificity control of `ADR-128` × **1** on the identical probe, so the zero discriminates. The contended sibling PR #5081 — which carries a *different* ADR-129 under `core/ADRs/` — was re-verified `OPEN` at the merge instant, confirming the Stage-9 reading that **this release merges first** and, per **ADR-115**, the renumber falls to the later claimant. Gate `G-EX9` does not apply.

**Release-issue auto-close did not fire, by design rather than by defect.** #101, #335 and #21 all read `OPEN` immediately post-merge. The release PR body carries no `Closes`/`Fixes` keyword — it uses safe cross-reference phrasing throughout, per the parser-clean PR-body discipline — so closure is Stage 13's to perform at close-out. Recorded here so a later reader does not mistake three open issues under a `DEPLOYED` row for a missed Phase B1 step.

#### Deployment Log v4.18

Pull request … was already merged` rather than a success message.** The merge is therefore established by the Phase B merge verification protocol and **not** by the command's output — `state == "MERGED"` **and** `mergeCommit.oid` non-null, merged at `2026-08-08T04:16:36Z`. **Outcome:** SUCCESS

**The version claimed is `v4.18`, and the two figures carried into this stage were both stale — the tag surface is what caught it.** The branch's provisional was `v4.14`; the gate's adapter reading was `v4.17`. Neither was claimable. At the claim instant the adapter recomputed next-free as **`v4.18`**, and the reason is recorded because a ledger-only check would have inverted it: probing `v4.17` across three arms returned **tag surface × 1 (CLAIMED)**, **`RELEASE_LOG` rows × 0**, **published GitHub Releases × 0** — against a control probe of the fabricated `v4.99` returning **0 on every arm**, so the arms discriminate. A sibling release had pushed the `v4.17` tag without yet writing its ledger row, so the ledger and the Releases surface both still read the slot FREE. **A ledger-only or Releases-only freeness check would have returned a false PROCEED and driven a collision at the CAS.** This is the second recorded instance of the tag-surface-only visibility window on this pipeline. `A.5.6b/c`'s carried-vs-recomputed comparison had no subject to compare — the plan is slug-primary per ADR-092 and carries `{{RELEASE_VERSION}}`, not a baked number (`v4.1[4-8]` × **0** in the plan file pre-stamp) — so freeness was proved directly by the three-arm probe instead. **Consequence for the ledger's own readability:** the `v4.17` row is absent above at the time of writing, so the version column reads `… v4.15, v4.16, v4.18`. That gap is the sibling's Stage-12 row, not a missing row of this release's, and it is recorded here so a later reader does not mistake it for one.

**`--stamp-slug` ran from worktree isolation, taking no primary-checkout access and making no direct-to-main push.** Following the v4.15 precedent, the Stage-12 chore branch was created **before** invoking the claim, so `_host_commit_push`'s `git push origin HEAD` landed the stamp on exactly the branch this chore PR carries and the stamp reaches `main` through PR review. `{{RELEASE_VERSION}} → v4.18` resolved (**0** unresolved tokens remaining, against a sensitivity arm of **2** resolved `v4.18` occurrences in the same file) and `release/releases/plans/hub-spoke-execution-safety_RELEASE_PLAN.md` renamed to `release/releases/plans/v4/v4.18_RELEASE_PLAN.md`, in stamp commit `1b0d5c01` riding this PR.

**A.5.7 fired, returned DUPLICATE, and the renumber ran — gate G-EX9 applies.** The contended number resolved **against** this release: PR #4979 (`release/102-specialist-role-coverage`) merged first at `c74de241`, and its `core/ADRs/ADR-127-domain-is-a-parameter-of-the-architect-role.md` took 127 on the mainline. Per ADR-115 a number is allocated at authorship but **claimed at merge**, so the later claimant moves. `renumber-adr.py --detect` reported `ANCHOR 127 (origin/main)` · `NEXT-FREE 128` · `ADR-127 … DUPLICATE MAINLINE next=128`; `--renumber 127 128 --apply` moved this release's record to `release/ADRs/ADR-128-version-tags-are-retained-not-reaped.md`, R6 verify clean, and `check-adr-numbers.py` returns **PASS (128 ADRs, contiguous 001..128, no duplicates)** on the reconciled tree. The record's lineage is now `120 → 121 → 123 → 127 → 128` — a **fifth** hop — with one provenance note per hop. **Both exclusion classes a line-identity check cannot distinguish from a live citation were held fixed and verified, not swept.** (1) *Another release's claim history*: the sibling's own ADR file, `core/specs/skill-consultation-map.md`, `release/skills/pmo-architect/**` and its release plan are **byte-identical to `origin/main`** after the move (`git diff origin/main -- <those paths>` empty) — they legitimately cite the ADR-127 that now exists. The R3 sweep is branch-diff-scoped, which excluded them structurally; no `--exclude-path` was needed. (2) *This record's own earlier-hop entries*: the `123 → 127` line in the § Renumber log and the `## Status` provenance notes are intact, and a new `127 → 128` entry was **appended**, not substituted — verified by counting `NNN (slug) → **ADR-NNN**` entries, which reads **20** with **zero duplicates**. **Dry-run/apply reporting discrepancy, recorded:** `--renumber 127 128` without `--apply` reports "would rewrite 2 × ADR-127 in `core/ADRs/README.md`", but the apply path rewrites **0** there — the dry-run prints a raw token count while the write path applies `is_historical_numbering_line`'s line-wise exemption. Read literally, the dry-run predicts falsifying two audit records. The prediction is wrong, not the write; it is logged as a reporting defect rather than left as a trap for the next reader.

**Pre-merge re-baseline — A.5.1–A.5.5 fired, and the drift was an order of magnitude larger than carried.** Divergence at the pre-merge instant was **34** commits, not the 3 this stage was briefed with — the sibling release `102-specialist-role-coverage` and the v4.16 close-out both landed in the interval. `git merge origin/main` ran into the release branch (**not** a rebase, which would have rewritten 32 commits), landing as `714cf450`. **One conflict, in `core/ADRs/README.md`**, resolved by hand as the file's own header requires — it self-declares a *curated thematic document, not an index*, so it is not projector-regenerable. Both branches had appended to the single-paragraph § Renumber log; the resolution is the **union**, verified structurally rather than by eye: parsing entries from each side gave 16 and 16 with 13 shared, **3 ours-only and 3 theirs-only**, and the resolved paragraph carries **19 with zero duplicates** and all six divergent entries present, against a fabricated-entry control returning absent. (Both records had independently traversed `120 → 121 → 123 → 127`, which is why a naive take-one-side resolution would have silently erased three real audit entries.) The sibling ADR index `release/ADRs/README.md` is a **derived** surface and was regenerated by `generate-adr-index.py`, never hand-edited. **Losslessness was verified at whole-tree scope, not per-file**, because the failure mode this guards against is a mainline rename dropped with no conflict raised: the expected post-merge path set was computed in advance as `origin/main` (**1,629**) ∪ branch additions (**16**) = **1,645**; the actual merged tree is **1,645** with **0 lost and 0 extra**. The specific hazard was live — mainline had renamed `release/releases/plans/methodology-fields-and-statuses_RELEASE_PLAN.md` into `plans/v4/v4.16_RELEASE_PLAN.md` — and the invariant confirms the rename target present and the rename source absent, with a known-absent control path correctly reading absent. CI was re-run to completion on the re-baselined head and again after a further in-scope commit landed mid-stage, returning **46/46 success** both times; the merge was taken only after that settle, never on a pending check. **CI roster counts were polled on `status != completed` and required stable across consecutive polls, never asserted against a remembered number** — the roster for this PR has legitimately read 45, 46, 48 and 62 at different instants, and a fixed assertion would have reported a catastrophic collapse where nothing was wrong.

**A concurrent commit landed on the release branch mid-stage and was not merged unreviewed.** After the re-baseline was pushed, head advanced from `6161ba2a` to `9d41b863` (`fix(hub-spoke): echo the run directory in relative form, never the resolved path`) and `mergeStateStatus` went `UNSTABLE`. The commit was read before proceeding: it narrows the Run-Directory Discipline's echo mandate to the `${SCRATCH_BASE}`-relative form, because the resolved form embeds the operator's OS username and the spoke output comment is a public surface — an in-scope public-surface privacy fix for this very milestone, carrying a negative test arm (A4b) asserting the absolute form is absent. CI was re-run to completion on that head and the merge was taken only after **46/46 success** and 9/9 required contexts re-verified.

**What this release does NOT claim.** The enforcement point ships **wired and inert**, exactly as the plan states, and this record makes no stronger claim. **The release does not claim the enforcement point has been observed firing.** Its negative control could not execute in-pipeline for either arm, because **three operator-executed preconditions** — the user-scope pre-tool-use wiring, the master security-hook switch, and the skill-edit hook's enforce mode — live outside the repository, are performed by the operator after this merge and before the milestone closes, and are named in full in the plan. The Edit-class arm in particular is **dormant on delivery**: its hook is workflow-class and a workflow-class hook exits without acting while the master switch is off. The proof obligation is specified precisely enough to execute and is **deferred, not waived**; the release's inference-vs-observation ledger ships in the corpus saying so. Reverting this merge does not unwind the preconditions — they are unwound by the operator, in the operator's own configuration.
